"TS11", "STRIDE": "Repudiation", "Scenario": "Changes to software, prompts or configuration on the unit are not recorded in tamper-evident logs, so a manipulation cannot be traced to an actor or a {"ID": "TS15", "STRIDE": "Spoofing", "Scenario": "Adversarial patches (printed patterns on vehicles, signs or clothing) make YOLOv5 report stopped vehicles or pedestrians that do not exist, sending events."},      {"ID": "TS24", "STRIDE": "Elevation of privilege", "Scenario": "Injected instructions make the agent framework perform actions beyond its role (e.g., invoking the message generation collects all hazard events and station positions, mapping sensor coverage and incident hotspots."         },         {           "id": "TL2-D1",           "stride": "Denial of Service",           "scenario": "Constant-carrier or reactive jamming of the sidelink blocks all vehicle-facing warnings in the coverage area while the roadside system continues to believe it is operating normally." devices eventually fail closed and stop accepting or sending safety messages."         },         {           "id": "TL3-E1",           "stride": "Elevation of Privilege",           "scenario": "Abuse of the device-management side of this channel (remote configuration, OTA update) allows an attacker to push malicious firmware to the whole RSU/OBU fleet."         }       \]     },     {       "asset": "Roadside ITS station platform (agent orchestration host, OS, configuration, model store, logs)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         {           "id": "TP1-S1",           "stride": "Spoofing",           "scenario": "Weak or shared service accounts between pipeline stages let any compromised component impersonate another, since the agent chain relies on implicit local trust."         },         {           "id": "TP1-T1",           "stride": "Tampering",           "scenario": "A malicious or unsigned software/OTA update replaces pipeline components or model artefacts, establishing persistent control over the detection logic."         },         {           "id": "TP1-T2",           "stride": "Tampering",           "scenario": "System clock manipulation (NTP spoofing) invalidates DENM timestamps, certificate validity checks and event ordering across the whole chain."         },         {           "id": "TP1-R1",           "stride": "Repudiation",           "scenario": "Logs are stored locally, unsigned and writable by the same processes they record, so an attacker with host access can erase the evidence of an intrusion or a suppressed hazard."         },         {           "id": "TP1-I1",           "stride": "Information Disclosure",           "scenario": "Stored frames, API keys, prompts and configuration files are unencrypted at rest; theft of the cabinet hardware yields credentials and personal data in the clear."         },         {           "id": "TP1-D1",           "stride": "Denial of Service",           "scenario": "Resource exhaustion (disk full from frame retention, GPU contention between YOLOv5, Depth Pro and agent runtimes) halts the pipeline without an externally visible failure signal."         },         {           "id": "TP1-E1",           "stride": "Elevation of Privilege",           "scenario": "A local privilege-escalation flaw or an exposed maintenance/SSH interface gives root on the station, which by design holds the right to request signatures for broadcast safety messages."         }       \]     }   \] } |

RSU (variable signs, PC5 radio, HSM)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T9.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "Rogue roadside transmitter clones the RSU identity and broadcasts fabricated hazard warnings; Sybil behaviour with multiple pseudonyms makes a single attacker look like several stations." },         { "id": "T9.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Physical access to the roadside cabinet enables firmware reflashing, sign-content manipulation or insertion of an inline device on the RSU-OBU path." },         { "id": "T9.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "HSM signing operations are not logged with operator identity, so unauthorised signing of DENMs cannot be attributed." },         { "id": "T9.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Extraction of private keys or key material from HSM side channels, memory dumps or an exposed PKCS\#11 interface compromises message authenticity." },         { "id": "T9.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "RF jamming of the ITS-G5/PC5 channel, channel-busy-ratio flooding, or backhaul/power interruption prevents warnings from reaching vehicles." },         { "id": "T9.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "Weakly protected remote management interface (SSH/web/OTA) gives full control of the RSU and access to the signing service." }       \]     },     {       "asset": "10. Central ITS-S / PKI and operator back end",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T10.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "Fraudulent enrolment request with forged canonical identity yields valid enrolment and authorisation tickets for an attacker-controlled station." },         { "id": "T10.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Manipulation or blocking of CTL/CRL distribution keeps revoked or rogue stations trusted by vehicles and RSUs." },         { "id": "T10.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "Certificate issuance and revocation decisions are not recorded in a tamper-evident audit log, so misissuance cannot be proven." },         { "id": "T10.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Correlation of enrolment records with pseudonym certificates de-anonymises vehicles and links trips to owners." },         { "id": "T10.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "DoS against the enrolment/authorisation endpoints prevents certificate renewal, so stations progressively lose the ability to sign or validate messages." },         { "id": "T10.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "Compromise of the AA/RA signing key allows the attacker to mint arbitrary valid certificates, collapsing the system root of trust." }       \]     },     {       "asset": "5G Uu link (RSU / agents \<-\> cloud and central ITS-S)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T11.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "False base station (rogue gNB) attracts the RSU modem and impersonates the network for the event-report and certificate channel." },         { "id": "T11.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Machine-in-the-middle on the cellular path alters event reports or MLLM responses where transport security is misconfigured." },         { "id": "T11.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "Event reports sent to the operator are not individually signed, so their origin and content can later be disputed." },         { "id": "T11.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Traffic analysis of encrypted uplink reveals when and where incidents occur, usable for targeting or insider trading of road data." },         { "id": "T11.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "Cellular jamming, SIM suspension or network congestion removes cloud reasoning and certificate updates from the roadside station." },         { "id": "T11.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "Baseband/modem firmware vulnerability is exploited over the air to gain execution on the communication module." }       \]     },     {       "asset": "PC5 sidelink (RSU \-\> OBU broadcast)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T12.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "Replay of a previously valid, correctly signed DENM at a different time or location to create phantom hazards and force unnecessary braking." },         { "id": "T12.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Selective relay/forwarding or bit-level corruption alters position or cause code in messages travelling through a malicious relay." },         { "id": "T12.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "Receivers do not retain the signed message that triggered a warning, so the cause of a reaction cannot be proven after a crash." },         { "id": "T12.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Passive eavesdropping on the broadcast channel enables long-term tracking of vehicles through pseudonym linkage." },         { "id": "T12.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "Channel jamming or deliberate congestion of the sidelink resource pool prevents safety warnings from being delivered in time." },         { "id": "T12.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "Protocol-stack vulnerability in the sidelink receiver is exploited by a crafted frame to execute code in the OBU." }       \]     },     {       "asset": "11. OBU (PC5 \+ Uu modem)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T13.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "Signature verification is skipped or misconfigured, so messages from uncertified senders are accepted as authentic ITS warnings." },         { "id": "T13.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Unsigned or downgradable over-the-air update installs modified OBU firmware that alters or suppresses warnings." },         { "id": "T13.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "No secure log of received, accepted and discarded messages, so the vehicle owner or manufacturer can deny reception of a valid warning." },         { "id": "T13.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Stored pseudonym-change history and received-message logs are read out, exposing the vehicle's past routes." },         { "id": "T13.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "Cryptographic DoS: flood of messages with invalid signatures exhausts verification capacity so genuine DENMs are dropped." },         { "id": "T13.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "Parser vulnerability in the ASN.1/security-envelope handling is used to reach the in-vehicle network from the communication unit." }       \]     },     {       "asset": "12. Vehicle computer (verification \+ warning logic)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T14.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "Injected internal bus messages imitate a verified DENM so the warning logic reacts to a non-existent roadside event." },         { "id": "T14.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Modification of the trust store, relevance filters or distance/time thresholds causes valid warnings to be discarded or irrelevant ones to be raised." },         { "id": "T14.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "Event data recorder entries for received warnings are missing or overwritable, preventing accident reconstruction." },         { "id": "T14.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Readout of cached positions, warnings and driver reactions via diagnostic access exposes behavioural data." },         { "id": "T14.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "Resource exhaustion in the verification/warning task delays alerts past the driver's reaction window." },         { "id": "T14.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "Lateral movement from the infotainment/connectivity domain into the safety-relevant domain due to insufficient domain separation." }       \]     },     {       "asset": "13. Driver HMI (visual \+ audio alert)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T15.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "A third-party application or paired device renders an alert that appears to originate from the ITS safety system." },         { "id": "T15.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Alert text, severity or priority is altered, or the audio channel is muted, so a critical accident warning is displayed as informational." },         { "id": "T15.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "No proof that a warning was actually presented to the driver, allowing the driver or the OEM to deny that the alert was shown." },         { "id": "T15.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Alert history visible on the HMI or exported to a companion app reveals the driver's route and stops." },         { "id": "T15.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "Alert flooding causes habituation and driver distraction, effectively disabling the safety function and creating a new hazard." },         { "id": "T15.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "HMI application permissions are abused to control unrelated vehicle functions or to suppress other safety notifications." }       \]     },     {       "asset": "Cryptographic material and API secrets (HSM keys, enrolment/authorisation tickets, cloud API keys)",       "threat\_scenarios": \[         { "id": "T16.1", "stride": "Spoofing", "scenario": "Stolen authorisation tickets let an attacker sign DENMs that every vehicle in range accepts as genuine." },         { "id": "T16.2", "stride": "Tampering", "scenario": "Trust anchor list on RSU or OBU is modified to include an attacker-controlled root." },         { "id": "T16.3", "stride": "Repudiation", "scenario": "Key usage is not tied to an operator identity or session, so malicious signing cannot be attributed." },         { "id": "T16.4", "stride": "Information Disclosure", "scenario": "Secrets stored in configuration files, container images or repository history are exfiltrated." },         { "id": "T16.5", "stride": "Denial of Service", "scenario": "Mass revocation triggered by an attacker, or expiry without renewal, leaves stations unable to sign or verify." },         { "id": "T16.6", "stride": "Elevation of Privilege", "scenario": "A single compromised credential grants both cloud inference and PKI enrolment rights due to missing separation of duties." }       \]     }   \] } |
